益生菌作为缓解禽类病毒感染的策略:免疫调节机制与治疗潜力

Probiotics as a Strategy for Mitigating Viral Infections in Poultry: Immunomodulatory Mechanisms and Therapeutic Potential.
作者： Elmer Nayra Ametefe, Daniel Saka, Juliet Nuerkie Anang, Lourraine Sena Doh, Leonard Kofi Adjorlolo
摘要中文翻译禽类病毒感染仍然是禽类养殖业中的持续挑战,导致显著的经济损失并可能对人类健康构成潜在威胁。涉及病原体的病毒感染频率不断上升,例如传染性支气管炎病毒(IBV)、新城疫病毒(NDV)和禽流感病毒(AIV),凸显了对有效干预措施的迫切需求。由于益生菌在增强免疫应答和建立健康肠道微生物群方面具有潜力,它们已成为一种有前景的策略,用于对抗这些病毒感染。通过在Google Scholar、Scopus和PubMed上使用关键搜索词,如'益生菌'、'乳酸杆菌'、'嗜酸乳杆菌'、'禽类'、'病毒感染'、'新城疫病毒(NDV)'及其他相关术语进行了文献检索。所选文献的纳入标准基于其与禽类益生菌干预措施的相关性、对免疫机制的关注以及对西非禽类养殖系统中常见病毒病原体的分析。本综述探讨了益生菌在减轻NDV、AIV和IBV对禽类影响中的作用,重点在于其免疫调节效应和抗病毒机制。嗜酸乳杆菌(Lactobacillus acidophilus)和植物乳杆菌(L. plantarum)在调节免疫参数和增强抗体反应方面显示出对抗NDV感染的有趣前景。同样,这些益生菌在对抗AIV方面也表现出抗病毒活性,促进免疫激活并降低病毒载量。植物乳杆菌在应对IBV病毒感染方面已显示出潜在的辅助作用,改善抗体反应和体液免疫。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述的重点是通过检查益生菌的免疫调节作用和 抗病毒药物 机制,使用益生菌减轻家禽病毒感染,特别是 禽流感 A 病毒。核心问题是益生菌是否可以作为一种有效策略,通过人畜共患 传播 来减少这些病毒对家禽健康和潜在人类健康的影响。
证据基础与方法
该综述使用“益生菌”、“乳杆菌”、“嗜酸菌”、“家禽”、“病毒感染”、“新城疫病毒 (NDV)”等术语和其他相关术语在 Google Scholar、Scopus 和 PubMed 上进行了文献检索。纳入标准基于与家禽益生菌干预措施的相关性、对免疫机制的关注以及对西非家禽养殖系统中常见病毒病原体的检查。
共识与结论
益生菌,特别是嗜酸乳杆菌和植物乳杆菌,已显示出调节免疫参数、增强抗体反应和减少针对 禽流感 A 病毒 (AIV) 的病毒载量的潜力。这些发现表明,益生菌可能是改善家禽 AIV 免疫防御的可行策略。
争议与缺口
该综述强调需要对益生菌针对特定 禽流感 亚型(例如 H5N1 和 H5N6)的作用进行更有针对性的研究。关于益生菌在预防人类 AIV 人畜共患 传播 方面的长期功效的证据有限,需要进一步研究来解决这些差距。
科研与实践启示
未来的研究应优先研究益生菌对特定 禽流感 亚型的功效及其在预防人畜共患 传播 中的作用。实际应用可能包括将益生菌融入家禽养殖实践中,以增强 免疫应答 并减少 AIV 暴发 的经济和公共卫生负担。

Probiotics as a Strategy for Mitigating Viral Infections in Poultry: Immunomodulatory Mechanisms and Therapeutic Potential.

Authors: Elmer Nayra Ametefe, Daniel Saka, Juliet Nuerkie Anang, Lourraine Sena Doh, Leonard Kofi Adjorlolo
Original abstract (English)
Poultry viral infections remain a persistent challenge for the poultry industry, causing significant economic losses and posing potential threats to human health. The escalating frequency of outbreaks involving pathogens, such as infectious bronchitis virus (IBV), Newcastle disease virus (NDV) and avian influenza virus (AIV), underscores the urgent need for effective interventions. Probiotics have gained attention as a promising strategy to combat these viral infections due to their potential in enhancing immune responses and establishing a healthy gut microbiome. A literature search was carried out on Google Scholar, Scopus and PubMed using key search terms, such as 'probiotics', 'Lactobacillus', 'acidophilus', 'poultry', 'viral infections', 'Newcastle Disease Virus (NDV)' and other relevant terms. Inclusion criteria for manuscripts used were based on the relevance to probiotic-based interventions in poultry, the focus on immunological mechanisms and the examination of viral pathogens commonly found in West African poultry farming systems. This review explores the role of probiotics in mitigating the effects of NDV, AIV and IBV in poultry, focusing on their immunomodulatory effects and antiviral mechanisms. Lactobacillus acidophilus, L. plantarum have shown an interesting promise in countering NDV infections by modulating immunological parameters and enhancing antibody responses. Similarly, these probiotics have exhibited antiviral activity against AIV, promoting immune activation and reducing viral loads. plantarum have emerged as potential adjuvants for IBV vaccines, improving antibody responses and humoural immunity.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the use of probiotics to mitigate viral infections in poultry, particularly avian influenza A viruses, by examining their immunomodulatory effects and antiviral mechanisms. The central question is whether probiotics can serve as an effective strategy for reducing the impact of these viruses on poultry health and potentially human health through zoonotic transmission.
Evidence base and method
The review conducted a literature search on Google Scholar, Scopus, and PubMed using terms like 'probiotics', 'Lactobacillus', 'acidophilus', 'poultry', 'viral infections', 'Newcastle Disease Virus (NDV)', and other relevant terms. Inclusion criteria were based on the relevance to probiotic-based interventions in poultry, the focus on immunological mechanisms, and the examination of viral pathogens common in West African poultry farming systems.
Consensus and conclusions
Probiotics, particularly Lactobacillus acidophilus and Lactobacillus plantarum, have shown potential in modulating immunological parameters, enhancing antibody responses, and reducing viral loads against avian influenza A viruses (AIV). These findings suggest that probiotics may be a viable strategy for improving immune defenses against AIV in poultry.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights the need for more targeted studies on probiotics' effects against specific avian influenza subtypes, such as H5N1 and H5N6. There is limited evidence on the long-term efficacy of probiotics in preventing zoonotic transmission of AIV to humans, and further research is required to address these gaps.
Research and practice implications
Future research should prioritize investigating the efficacy of probiotics against specific avian influenza subtypes and their role in preventing zoonotic transmission. Practical applications may include integrating probiotics into poultry farming practices to enhance immune responses and reduce the economic and public health burden of AIV outbreaks.

和泉平原起重机越冬地点的集成 HPAIV 监测

Integrated HPAIV surveillance at a crane wintering site in the Izumi Plain
作者： Mana Esaki, Kosuke Okuya, Makoto Ozawa
摘要中文翻译日本和泉平原是濒临灭绝的鹤的主要越冬地,毗邻密集的家禽生产区,为 禽流感 病毒 (AIV) 监测 创造了重要的接触面。这篇评论综合了十多年的环境和野生鸟类 监测,重点关注从人工鹤栖息处收集的水。最初,从栖息水中分离病毒效率低下,产生的分离株很少。2018/19 季节引入的修改程序显着提高了分离效率,该季节产生了 44 种低致病性 禽流感 病毒 (LPAIV)。传染性分离株可用性的增加使得全 基因组 分析能够揭示亚型多样性、重配和基因型更新,将栖息水 基因组 从零星检测转变为表征 AIV 循环的工具。在 2022/23 年高致病性 禽流感 (HPAI) 暴发 期间,广泛的起重机 死亡率 与从栖息水中分离的有限 HPAIV 形成对比,突出了单独环境 监测 的局限性。整合环境、基于 宿主 的血清学和基因组数据可以更全面地了解 HPAIV 动态,并加强 监测 在高密度野生鸟类聚集地的作用。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注在日本泉平原(毗邻家禽生产区)越冬的鹤中的 禽流感 A 病毒 (AIV) 及其 监测。核心问题是人工鹤栖息地的水环境 监测 如何有助于了解 AIV 循环和动态。
证据基础与方法
该评论综合了十多年的环境和野生鸟类 监测 数据,特别是从人工鹤栖息地收集的水。这些方法包括病毒分离、2018/19赛季引入的修改程序以及用于表征AIV循环的全基因组分析。
共识与结论
修改后的程序显着提高了病毒分离效率,导致2018/19赛季鉴定出44种低致病性禽流感病毒(LPAIV)。全 基因组 分析揭示了亚型多样性、重配和基因型更新,将栖息水 基因组 转变为表征 AIV 循环的工具。
争议与缺口
在 2022/23 HPAI 暴发 期间,观察到了大量起重机 死亡率,但检测到 HPAIV 与栖息水的分离有限,突出了环境 监测 的局限性。需要集成多个数据源以更好地了解 HPAIV 动态。
科研与实践启示
建议整合环境、基于 宿主 的血清学和基因组数据,以更全面地了解 HPAIV 动态,并加强高密度野鸟聚集地的 监测。

Integrated HPAIV surveillance at a crane wintering site in the Izumi Plain

Authors: Mana Esaki, Kosuke Okuya, Makoto Ozawa
Original abstract (English)
The Izumi Plain, Japan, is a major wintering site for endangered cranes adjacent to intensive poultry production, creating an important interface for avian influenza virus (AIV) surveillance. This Review synthesizes more than a decade of environmental and wild-bird surveillance, focusing on water collected from artificial crane roosts. Initially, virus isolation from roost water was inefficient, yielding few isolates. A modified procedure introduced in the 2018/19 season markedly improved isolation efficiency, yielding 44 low pathogenicity avian influenza viruses (LPAIVs) that season. The increased availability of infectious isolates enabled whole-genome analyses that revealed subtype diversity, reassortment, and genotype turnover, transforming roost-water surveillance from sporadic detection into a tool for characterizing AIV circulation. During the 2022/23 high pathogenicity avian influenza (HPAI) outbreak, extensive crane mortality contrasted with limited HPAIV isolation from roost water, highlighting the limitations of environmental surveillance alone. Integrating environmental, host-based, serological, and genomic data provides a more comprehensive understanding of HPAIV dynamics and strengthens surveillance at high-density wild-bird aggregation sites.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on avian influenza A viruses (AIVs) and their surveillance in the context of cranes wintering in the Izumi Plain, Japan, which is adjacent to poultry production. The central question is how environmental surveillance of water from artificial crane roosts can contribute to understanding AIV circulation and dynamics.
Evidence base and method
The review synthesizes more than a decade of environmental and wild-bird surveillance data, specifically water collected from artificial crane roosts. The methods include virus isolation, modified procedures introduced in the 2018/19 season, and whole-genome analyses to characterize AIV circulation.
Consensus and conclusions
The modified procedure significantly improved virus isolation efficiency, leading to the identification of 44 low pathogenicity avian influenza viruses (LPAIVs) in the 2018/19 season. Whole-genome analyses revealed subtype diversity, reassortment, and genotype turnover, transforming roost-water surveillance into a tool for characterizing AIV circulation.
Controversies and gaps
During the 2022/23 HPAI outbreak, extensive crane mortality was observed, but limited HPAIV isolation from roost water was detected, highlighting the limitations of environmental surveillance alone. There is a need for integrating multiple data sources to better understand HPAIV dynamics.
Research and practice implications
Integrating environmental, host-based, serological, and genomic data is recommended to provide a more comprehensive understanding of HPAIV dynamics and to strengthen surveillance at high-density wild-bird aggregation sites.

C 补充信息学术文献 · 综述C supplementaryAcademic literature · Review

人与动物界面上的牛相关病毒:人畜共患风险、跨物种溢出 动态和对健康的影响

Bovine-Associated Viruses at the Human–Animal Interface: Zoonotic Risks, Spillover Dynamics, and One Health Implications
作者： Chang-Qing Yu, Qing-Yuan Xu, Dong-Ping Xu, Qi Zhang, Ming-Yi Li, Bin Zhang
摘要中文翻译牛是许多人畜共患病毒的重要生态储存宿主和放大宿主,从而在其维持、进化和种间传播中发挥核心作用。在这里,我们简要评估了与牛相关的六种具有重要公共卫生相关性的病毒病原体,即牛痘病毒、牛冠状病毒、甲型流感病毒(H5N1 进化枝 2.3.4.4b)、牛轮状病毒、牛白血病病毒和 SARS-CoV-2。本综述重点关注病毒特征、宿主 向性以及可能通过直接接触、环境暴露和食源性 传播 等途径促进 跨物种溢出 的机制。它进一步强调了人与动物界面上病原体交换的双向性质,其中牛可能充当具有潜在公共卫生影响的病毒变异体的来源,尽管所讨论的病毒之间的风险差异很大。此外,我们讨论了综合 监测 和 One Health 干预措施以减轻这些 传播 风险的必要性,强调牛是全球健康安全框架内有价值的哨兵物种。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
禽流感 A 病毒、禽源人畜共患感染和合格的禽亚型是本次综述的重点。核心问题是探索这些病毒在人与动物界面上的人畜共患风险、跨物种溢出 动态以及“同一个健康”的影响。
证据基础与方法
该综述评估了与牛相关的六种具有重大公共卫生相关性的病毒病原体,包括 禽流感 A 病毒(H5N1 进化枝 2.3.4.4b)。证据来自摘要,摘要概述了该综述的重点是病毒特征、宿主 趋向性和 跨物种溢出 机制。该审查没有明确报告荟萃分析或共识过程。
共识与结论
该评论强调,禽流感 A 病毒,特别是 H5N1,对于人畜共患病毒的维持和进化至关重要。它强调了牛作为生态 储存宿主 的作用,并放大了这些病毒的 宿主,从而促成了它们的种间 传播。该综述还强调了了解人与动物界面病原体交换的双向性质的重要性。
争议与缺口
审查指出,禽流感 A 病毒传播到人类的风险在所讨论的病毒中差异很大。目前缺乏关于 跨物种溢出 的具体机制以及 传播 通过直接接触、环境暴露和食源性 传播 等不同途径传播的程度的详细信息。
科研与实践启示
该审查呼吁采取综合的 监测 和 One Health 干预措施,以减轻 传播 A 病毒的 传播 风险。它强调需要进一步研究 跨物种溢出 的机制,并制定有针对性的策略来监测和控制动物和人类群体中的这些病毒。

Bovine-Associated Viruses at the Human–Animal Interface: Zoonotic Risks, Spillover Dynamics, and One Health Implications

Authors: Chang-Qing Yu, Qing-Yuan Xu, Dong-Ping Xu, Qi Zhang, Ming-Yi Li, Bin Zhang
Original abstract (English)
Cattle serve as critical ecological reservoirs and amplifying hosts for numerous zoonotic viruses, thereby playing a central role in their maintenance, evolution, and interspecies transmission. Here, we briefly evaluate six viral pathogens of significant public health relevance linked to cattle, namely cowpox virus, bovine coronavirus, influenza A virus (H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b), bovine rotavirus, bovine leukemia virus, and SARS-CoV-2. This review focuses on advances in viral characterization, host tropism, and the mechanisms that may facilitate spillover through routes such as direct contact, environmental exposure, and foodborne transmission. It further highlights the bidirectional nature of pathogen exchange at the human-animal interface, where cattle may act as a source of viral variants with potential public health implications, though the risk varies considerably among the viruses discussed. Moreover, we discuss the imperative for integrated surveillance and One Health interventions to mitigate these transmission risks, highlighting cattle as valuable sentinel species within global health security frameworks.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Avian influenza A viruses, avian-origin zoonotic infections, and qualified avian subtypes are the focus of this review. The central question is to explore the zoonotic risks, spillover dynamics, and One Health implications of these viruses at the human–animal interface.
Evidence base and method
The review evaluates six viral pathogens of significant public health relevance linked to cattle, including avian influenza A virus (H5N1 clade 2.3.4.4b). The evidence is derived from the abstract, which outlines the review's focus on viral characterization, host tropism, and spillover mechanisms. The review does not explicitly report on meta-analysis or consensus processes.
Consensus and conclusions
The review highlights that avian influenza A viruses, particularly H5N1, are critical in maintaining and evolving zoonotic viruses. It emphasizes the role of cattle as ecological reservoirs and amplifying hosts for these viruses, contributing to their interspecies transmission. The review also underscores the importance of understanding the bidirectional nature of pathogen exchange at the human–animal interface.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes that the risk of avian influenza A viruses spilling over into humans varies considerably among the viruses discussed. There is a lack of detailed information on the specific mechanisms of spillover and the extent of transmission through different routes such as direct contact, environmental exposure, and foodborne transmission.
Research and practice implications
The review calls for integrated surveillance and One Health interventions to mitigate the transmission risks of avian influenza A viruses. It emphasizes the need for further research on the mechanisms of spillover and the development of targeted strategies to monitor and control these viruses in both animal and human populations.

华盛顿报告 2026 年美国首例禽流感死亡病例;卫生官员称公众面临的风险很低 - CNN

Washington reports first US bird flu death of 2026; health officials say risk to the public is low - CNN
新闻简报华盛顿州亚基马县的一名人员在接触受感染的奶牛后感染禽流感死亡,这是美国 2026 年第一例禽流感死亡病例。卫生官员证实了这起死亡事件,并指出这是自 2024 年 2 月以来美国的第三例死亡病例。该人有潜在的健康问题,但没有提供具体细节。亚基马县卫生区于 2026 年 11 月 1 日报告了这起死亡事件,并正在监测接触者以防止进一步传播。这一事件是美国禽流感病例大趋势的一部分,自 2024 年以来已记录了 70 多起人间病例。大多数病例都是轻度和死端感染,没有记录到人与人之间的 传播。疾病预防控制中心已获悉该病例,但尚未将其纳入禽流感仪表板中。官员们强调,公众面临的风险仍然很低,该州正在敦促乳制品工人采取预防措施并报告症状。华盛顿州的 暴发 与 H5N1 病毒有关,H5N1 病毒自 2026 年 10 月初以来一直在奶牛群中传播。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该事件于2026年11月2日报道。该人于2026年11月1日死亡,第一例病例于2026年10月下旬发现。
地点与对象
该事件发生在美国华盛顿州亚基马县,涉及一名有基础健康问题并接触受感染奶牛的人。
事件
华盛顿州亚基马县的一名居民因接触受感染的奶牛而感染禽流感,现已重病死亡。这是 2026 年美国首例已知的 禽流感 死亡病例,也是 2024 年 2 月以来的第三例。
规模、影响与风险
华盛顿州已报告三例人类病例,其中一例死亡。自 2024 年以来,美国疾病控制和预防中心 (CDC) 已记录了 70 多人感染禽流感病例。卫生官员表示,大多数病例都是轻微病例,而且都是死胡同感染,没有人传人 传播 的记录。公众面临的风险仍然很低。
应对、状态与不确定性
亚基马县卫生官员正在密切监视死者的接触者,以确保不再出现 传播。该州敦促乳品厂工作人员佩戴个人防护装备并报告症状。疾病预防控制中心已获悉所有三例病例,但尚未将其纳入其禽流感仪表板中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Washington reports first US bird flu death of 2026; health officials say risk to the public is low - CNN

News Brief
A person in Yakima County, Washington, who contracted bird flu after exposure to infected dairy cows has died, marking the first US bird flu death of 2026. Health officials confirmed the death, noting it is the third in the US since February 2024. The individual had underlying health conditions, though specifics were not provided. The Yakima County Health District reported the death on November 1, 2026, and is monitoring contacts to prevent further spread. This incident occurred as part of a broader trend of bird flu cases in the US, with over 70 human cases recorded since 2024. Most cases have been mild and dead-end infections, with no documented human-to-human transmission. The CDC has been informed of the case but has not included it on its bird flu dashboard. Officials emphasize that the risk to the public remains low, and the state is urging dairy workers to take precautions and report symptoms. The outbreak in Washington has been linked to H5N1, which has been circulating in dairy herds since early October 2026.
Original source text (English)

Washington reports first US bird flu death of 2026; health officials say risk to the public is low Respiratory viruses Agriculture See all topics Facebook Tweet Email Link Threads Link Copied! A microscope image shows influenza A virus subtype H5N1, commonly known as bird flu. Three cases have been reported in humans in Washington state, including one death. CDC/NIAID/AP A person in Yakima County, Washington, who caught bird flu after being exposed to infected dairy cows has died following a severe illness, county health officials said Sunday. This is the first known person in the US to die from complications from avian influenza in 2026 and the third in the US to die from bird flu since February 2024, when the virus jumped into dairy cattle for the first time. On October 31, the Yakima County Health District reported that a person who had come into contact with infected cattle was hospitalized with a severe case of bird flu. The person had underlying health conditions, but the county did not specify what they were. On November 1, the county reported that the person had died. Yakima County health officials said they are closely monitoring the contacts of the person who died to ensure that they didn’t pass the infection to anyone else. Ad Feedback The US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention has recorded bird flu cases in more than 70 people in the US since 2024. Most of the people who’ve become ill have worked on farms with infected cattle or poultry. Health officials say that most cases have been mild and all have been so-called dead-end infections, in which a virus jumps from an animal to a person but doesn’t spread farther. No human-to-human transmission of bird flu has been documented in the US. Health officials say the risk to the public remains low. Related article General view of Heard Island And Mcdonald Islands. Courtesy Australian Antarctic Division/Doug Thost Thousands of baby seals died on two remote sub-Antarctic islands. Scientists now think they know why 2 min read Three cases have been identified in people in the Yakima County Health District this year: a mild case identified last month, the person who died and a third case reported over the weekend. All three Washington cases have occurred since mid-October and resulted from exposure to infected cows. The third case, like the first case, has mild symptoms and has not been hospitalized, county health district officials said. State health officials said they have reported all three cases to the CDC. So far, the agency has not included them on its bird flu dashboard. Tracking bird flu cases in humans Since October 8, 33 dairy herds infected with the H5N1 bird flu virus have been reported in Washington. The state is urging people who work at the dairies to wear personal protective equipment and report their symptoms if they feel ill. Dr. Dave Atteberry, the public health officer for the Yakima health district, said the dairies with infected cows were found in early October through milk testing by the Washington State Department of Agriculture and the US Department of Agriculture. Public health workers with the county then reached out to the affected farms and enrolled dairy workers in a text-based health screening tool that checks daily for symptoms including cough, fever or red eyes. If the workers report any, the health department follows up with additional questions, Atteberry told CNN last month. “We’re throwing a large net so that we capture everybody that that potentially could be getting exposed, and then we’re able to focus our attention on those individuals that are exposed, and then we get them treated,” Atteberry said last month. On October 15, a dairy worker in the county tested positive for mild H5N1. It was the first human case reported in nearly a year. Related article Aster, a mink rescued from a fur farm, runs around the home of Linda Maretich, where Rocky Mountain Mink Refuge is based, in Saratoga Springs, Utah, on Sept. 26, 2026. Aster was brought to the refuge in 2024 with neck and abdominal injuries. Julie Leopo for ProPublica Bird flu is raging on a mink farm. It could spark a human pandemic, experts say, but officials won’t act. 16 min read Not all states are approaching bird flu the same way. ProPublica recently reported that Utah’s Department of Agriculture and Food agreed to share information about bird flu outbreaks on farms as long as health officials didn’t take steps to track workers’ health. This patchwork state-by-state approach to monitoring bird flu infections in people is concerning, said Dr. Jennifer Nuzzo, who directs the Pandemic Center at Brown University’s School of Public Health. “I just think that our our picture of H5N1 is the murkiest it’s ever been,” Nuzzo said. Nuzzo said this bird flu uptick at dairies in Washington, which has coincided with the very early circulation of seasonal influenza on the Pacific Coast, sets the table for the H5N1 virus to swap pieces of its genome with seasonal flu viruses. A big change like that could potentially give the virus the tools it needs to better infect humans. “That is the bad scenario. So when you have an uptick in bird flu activity corresponding with human flu season, that just increases my worry even more that we could have a mix that we don’t want to have,” she said. Atteberry said farm workers who test positive are given antiviral medication with the goal of treating their symptoms as well as lowering their viral load to reduce the chance they could pass it to others. “Even though we haven’t seen that biologically be possible yet,” Atteberry said. “We’re doing it out of an abundance of caution.” Respiratory viruses Agriculture See all topics Facebook Tweet Email Link Threads Link Copied! B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

华盛顿州报告奶牛首次出现禽流感 暴发 - 霍特科姆县

Washington State Reports First Bird Flu Outbreak in Dairy Cows - Whatcom County
新闻简报华盛顿州报告了首例奶牛感染禽流感 暴发,发生在亚基马县和本顿县。华盛顿州农业部 (WSDA) 和亚基马卫生区确认了 暴发,这标志着该州奶牛群中出现首例已知禽流感病例。亚基马县发现了三例 禽流感 人类病例,其中一名患有基础健康问题的人死亡。这些人在接触受感染的牛后生病了。目前美国尚无已知的人传人 传播。WSDA 正在与受影响的农场合作调查和测试牲畜,而卫生部和当地机构则监测农场工人并与联邦合作伙伴一起跟踪情况。普通公众感染 禽流感 的风险仍然较低,但与受感染动物密切接触的人风险较高。预防措施包括避免接触 野生鸟类、患病或死亡的动物及其粪便,以及隔离受影响的牲畜并向有关当局报告患病动物。
查看新闻五要素
时间
华盛顿州农业部 (WSDA) 和亚基马卫生区宣布了亚基马县和本顿县的 暴发。第一例人间病例发生在 2024 年 10 月,最新的人间病例于 2026 年在亚基马县确诊。
地点与对象
暴发 发生在华盛顿州亚基马县和本顿县,影响奶牛和与受感染牛接触过的亚基马县居民。
事件
亚基马县和本顿县的奶牛中报告了 禽流感 (H5N1) 暴发。亚基马县居民中确认了三例 禽流感 人类病例,其中一名有基础健康问题的人死亡。
规模、影响与风险
确诊三例人类病例,其中一人死亡。暴发 已被确定为华盛顿州奶牛群中的第一批。普通公众感染 禽流感 的风险仍然较低,但与受感染动物密切接触的人的风险较高。
应对、状态与不确定性
WSDA 正在调查和测试患病牲畜,而卫生部和当地公共卫生机构正在监测农场工人并与联邦合作伙伴跟踪情况。暴发 正在积极调查中,对公众的风险被认为较低。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Washington State Reports First Bird Flu Outbreak in Dairy Cows - Whatcom County

News Brief
Washington State has reported its first bird flu outbreak in dairy cattle, occurring in Yakima and Benton counties. The Washington State Department of Agriculture (WSDA) and Yakima Health District confirmed the outbreak, which marks the first known cases of bird flu in the state's dairy herds. Three human cases of avian influenza were identified in Yakima County, including one death in a person with underlying health conditions. These individuals became ill after contact with infected cattle. There is no known human-to-human transmission in the U.S. at this time. The WSDA is working with affected farms to investigate and test livestock, while the DOH and local agencies monitor farm workers and track the situation alongside federal partners. The risk of avian influenza infection to the general public remains low, but those with close contact with infected animals are at higher risk. Preventive measures include avoiding contact with wild birds, sick or dead animals, and their droppings, as well as isolating affected livestock and reporting sick animals to the appropriate authorities.

Your Government County Services Safety & Justice Home News Flash Washington State Reports First Bird Flu Outbreak in Dairy Cows News Flash Washington State Reports First Bird Flu Outbreak in Dairy Cows The Washington State Department of Agriculture (WSDA) and the Yakima Health District recently announced an avian influenza (H5N1) outbreak in dairy cattle across Yakima and Benton counties. These mark the first known cases of bird flu in Washington dairy herds. After detecting the virus in cattle, the Yakima Health District has confirmed 3 human cases of avian influenza among Yakima County residents, including one death in a person with underlying health conditions. The individuals became ill after contact with infected cattle. There is no known human-to-human transmission in the United States at this time. WSDA is working with affected farms to investigate and test sick livestock, while DOH and local public health agencies monitor farm workers and track the situation alongside federal partners. “This latest outbreak highlights the need to stay cautious,” said Dr. Amy Harley, Health Officer for Whatcom County Health and Community Services. “Dairy and poultry workers, backyard flock owners, hunters, and anyone who comes into contact with wildlife should take precautions to protect themselves and prevent the spread of bird flu.” So far this year, Whatcom County has no reported cases of bird flu in humans, farm animals, or backyard flocks. About Avian Influenza (H5N1) Highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI), often called "bird flu," is a virus common in birds worldwide that can cause serious illness in birds. HPAI can also infect other animals, including wild animals and livestock. While human cases of H5N1 are rare and typically happen through close contact with infected animals without proper protective gear, sporadic cases have been reported in the U.S. since 2022. Most U.S. cases have been mild, though a small number have required hospitalization or resulted in severe illness or death. Washington's first human case occurred in October 2024 from close contact with birds at a poultry farm. The risk of avian influenza infection to the general public in Washington State remains low. The greatest influenza risk in Washington is seasonal influenza; the seasonal flu vaccine is recommended for everyone six months and older. Prevent the Spread of Bird Flu Do not touch wild birds or wild animals. Wild birds and other animals can carry avian influenza even if they appear healthy. Avoid contact with sick or dead birds or animals. Avoid contact with bird droppings or contaminated surfaces (e.g., bird feeders). Reporting sick or dead animals in Washington depends on the type of animal: Domestic birds (chickens & ducks): Report to the WSDA Avian Health Program via their online form, or call 1-800-606-3056. Livestock (including cattle): Isolate affected animals and contact your veterinarian. Report sick livestock to WSDA at 360-902-1878, or email ahealth@agr.wa.gov. Wild birds: Report sick or dead wild birds to the Washington Department of Fish & Wildlife via their online form. People with recreational or job-related exposure to infected birds, cattle, or other infected animals, or their environments, should take extra precautions, including wearing recommended personal protective equipment. Do not eat or drink raw or undercooked food, such as unpasteurized (raw) milk or raw cheeses, or raw meats. Do not let your pets eat or drink raw milk or other dairy products, raw pet foods, or raw meat. Get the seasonal flu vaccine. Although it does not prevent bird flu, it reduces the risk of co-infection with human and avian influenza viruses. Although the season has started early, it's not too late to benefit from the flu vaccine. Protect Your Backyard Flock Wild birds can spread bird flu to pet birds, including chickens. Backyard flock owners are strongly encouraged to take proactive steps to protect their birds and reduce the risk of spreading the virus: Cover chicken coops and runs to prevent contact with wild birds. Wear dedicated clothing and shoes when working with birds to reduce cross-contamination risk. Wash your hands before and after working with birds. Avoid all contact with wild birds or shared water sources by covering ponds with netting or fencing off areas where domestic birds can't access them. Change food and water daily. Store all feed in airtight containers. Clean and disinfect equipment that comes into contact with birds or droppings. After visiting places with poultry, change your clothing and clean your footwear before caring for your birds. For more information on avian flu, visit the avian influenza page on the WA DOH website. Time
The Washington State Department of Agriculture (WSDA) and Yakima Health District announced the outbreak in Yakima and Benton counties. The first human case occurred in October 2024, and the latest human cases were confirmed in Yakima County in 2026.
Location and population
The outbreak occurred in Yakima and Benton counties, Washington State, affecting dairy cattle and residents of Yakima County who had contact with infected cattle.
Event
An avian influenza (H5N1) outbreak was reported in dairy cattle in Yakima and Benton counties. Three human cases of avian influenza were confirmed among Yakima County residents, including one death in a person with underlying health conditions.
Scale, impact and risk
Three human cases were confirmed, with one death. The outbreak has been identified as the first in Washington State dairy herds. The risk of avian influenza infection to the general public remains low, but the risk for those with close contact with infected animals is higher.
Response, status and uncertainty
The WSDA is investigating and testing sick livestock, while the DOH and local public health agencies are monitoring farm workers and tracking the situation with federal partners. The outbreak is under active investigation, and the risk to the general public is considered low.

亚基马县居民因接触乳制品而死于禽流感,这是今年美国首例死亡病例 - Global Biodefense

Bird Flu Kills Yakima County Resident Exposed at Dairy, First U.S. Death This Year - Global Biodefense
新闻简报美国华盛顿州亚基马县一名居民死于禽流感,这是美国今年首例死亡病例。该个体是在一家乳品厂暴露的,引起了人们对人畜共患 传播 从 禽流感 传染给人类的担忧。Global Biodefense 于 2026 年 11 月 3 日报道了该事件。虽然死亡已得到确认,但没有提供具体病例或暴露计数。该风险并未明确说明,但该事件强调了 禽流感 传播给人类的可能性。应对措施包括正在进行的调查和监控,但报告中没有概述详细的行动。该病例凸显了保持警惕预防动物源性人类感染的重要性。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bird Flu Kills Yakima County Resident Exposed at Dairy, First U.S. Death This Year - Global Biodefense

News Brief
A Yakima County resident in Washington, United States, has died from bird flu, marking the first U.S. death of the year. The individual was exposed at a dairy facility, raising concerns about zoonotic transmission from avian influenza to humans. The event was reported by Global Biodefense on November 3, 2026. While the death is confirmed, no specific case or exposure counts are provided. The risk is not explicitly stated, but the incident underscores the potential for avian influenza to spread to humans. The response includes ongoing investigation and monitoring, though no detailed actions are outlined in the report. This case highlights the importance of vigilance in preventing human infections from animal sources.

皮尔斯县商业家禽设施检测到禽流感 - 塔科马-皮尔斯县卫生局

Bird flu detected at commercial poultry facility in Pierce County - Tacoma-Pierce County Health Department
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 5 日,塔科马-皮尔斯县卫生部门报告称,皮尔斯县的一家商业家禽养殖场检测到禽流感。该事件于 2026 年 11 月 6 日发布。报告没有具体说明病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数,也没有提及任何卫生系统影响或运营影响。风险级别没有明确说明。卫生部门已发布有关检测的通知,但没有提供有关调查、检测、隔离、接触者追踪、预防或 治疗 措施的详细信息。响应状态正在调查中。

时间
该事件于2026年11月5日报道,发布日期为2026年11月6日。
地点与对象
该事件发生在皮尔斯县的一家商业家禽设施,该设施属于塔科马-皮尔斯县卫生局管辖范围。
事件
据塔科马-皮尔斯县卫生部门报告,皮尔斯县的一家商业家禽设施发现了禽流感。
规模、影响与风险

应对、状态与不确定性

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bird flu detected at commercial poultry facility in Pierce County - Tacoma-Pierce County Health Department

News Brief
On November 5, 2026, the Tacoma-Pierce County Health Department reported the detection of bird flu at a commercial poultry facility in Pierce County. The event was published on November 6, 2026. The report does not specify the number of cases, deaths, or exposures, nor does it mention any health-system effects or operational effects. The risk level is not explicitly stated. The health department has issued a notice about the detection, but details on the investigation, testing, isolation, contact tracing, prevention, or treatment measures are not provided. The status of the response is under investigation.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

犹他水貂 暴发:病毒学家称 H5N1 在美国农业部的注视下从牛身上溜走 - BigGo Finance

Utah Mink Outbreak: Virologists Say H5N1 Slipped From Cattle While USDA Watched - BigGo Finance
新闻简报2026 年 8 月,在犹他州农场的水貂中发现了 H5N1 暴发,从基因上追溯到适应牛的 B3.13 毒株,而不是 野生鸟类。该病毒已在未被发现的情况下传播了大约六周,最近的共同祖先可追溯到 2026 年 7 月 4 日。暴发 强调了 H5N1 适应哺乳动物的风险,可能导致人类适应。美国农业部没有对暴露的工人实施扑杀或监控,也没有 疫苗接种 或水貂损失补偿机制。监测 差距很大,在初始检测后没有进一步采集样本。暴发 引发了人们对该病毒在不同哺乳动物之间传播的能力以及缺乏有效公共卫生措施的担忧。作者认为,该病毒可以作为人类和禽类病毒株之间重配的载体,增加未来 暴发 的风险。这种情况凸显了跟踪和应对畜牧业 高致病性禽流感 的挑战,特别是在水貂养殖业,由于国内对水貂毛皮的需求不足,几乎没有采取 高致病性禽流感 或干预的动力。更广泛的影响包括需要改进 监测,以及如果在哺乳动物 宿主 中不加以控制,该病毒有可能进化成更严重的形式。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发 于 2026 年 8 月被发现,通过基因分析确定最近的共同祖先于 2026 年 7 月 4 日,并于 2026 年 8 月 7 日采集了第一批样本。十天后,即 2026 年 8 月 17 日,该检测得到确认。据报道,暴发 于 2026 年 9 月 29 日仍在进行中。(N1、N15、N16、N17、 N18)。
地点与对象
暴发 事件发生在犹他州,特别是在盐湖县的一个水貂养殖场。受影响的种群包括农场的水貂,它们是食肉动物,经常喂食家禽产品。
事件
犹他水貂中的 H5N1 暴发 在遗传上可追溯到适应牛的 B3.13 品系,而不是 野生鸟类。人们发现该病毒在未被发现的情况下传播了大约六周,然后才被发现。(N1、N9、N19、N20、N21、N22、N23)。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 涉及 B3.13 基因型的 10 个完整 基因组 序列,全部嵌套在 2026 牛组内。共享 突变(PB1 K388R)和牛 突变(PB2 M631L)的存在表明该病毒已适应哺乳动物。风险在于该病毒可能会适应人类并在人与人之间传播。(N1、N9、N19、N20、N21、N22、N23、N31、N33、N34、N35、N36、N37、N38、N39、N43、N44、N45、N47、N48、N49、N50、N51、N52)。
应对、状态与不确定性
美国农业部没有下令扑杀或监控暴露的农场工人。没有机制授权 疫苗接种 或补偿生产者的水貂损失。监测 漏洞已得到承认,但病毒来源仍未确定。(N23、N27、N28、N33、N34、N35、N36、N37、N38、N39、N45)。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Utah Mink Outbreak: Virologists Say H5N1 Slipped From Cattle While USDA Watched - BigGo Finance

News Brief
In August 2026, an H5N1 outbreak was discovered in mink at a Utah farm, traced genetically to the cattle-adapted B3.13 strain, not wild birds. The virus had been circulating undetected for about six weeks, with the most recent common ancestor dated to July 4, 2026. The outbreak highlights the risk of H5N1 adapting to mammals, potentially leading to human adaptation. The USDA did not implement culling or monitoring of exposed workers, and there is no mechanism for vaccination or compensation for mink losses. The surveillance gaps are significant, with no further samples taken after the initial detection. The outbreak raises concerns about the virus's ability to spread between different mammalian hosts and the lack of effective public health measures. The authors suggest that the virus could serve as a vessel for reassortment between human and avian strains, increasing the risk of future outbreaks. The situation underscores the challenges in tracking and responding to highly pathogenic avian influenza in livestock, particularly in the mink farming industry, where there is little incentive for surveillance or intervention due to the lack of domestic demand for mink pelts. The broader implications include the need for improved surveillance and the potential for the virus to evolve into a more severe form if left unchecked in mammalian hosts.
Original source text (English)
Ask AIReal-time News Market Earnings CallEvent Calendar More Earnings CallEvent CalendarWatchlistPodcastMarket InfluencersMore Ask AIReal-time News WatchlistHoldingsMarketEarnings CallEvent CalendarPodcastMarket Influencers Utah Mink Outbreak: Virologists Say H5N1 Slipped From Cattle While USDA Watched 2026-11-01 05:08 (GMT+0) Add preferred source An H5N1 outbreak in Utah mink has been traced genetically to the cattle-adapted B3.13 strain now circulating in US dairy herds — not to wild birds — revealing a virus that moved undetected for roughly six weeks and a surveillance system that has since stopped looking. Analysis of the outbreak preprint on the virology podcast TWiV, by hosts Rich Condit and Alan Dove alongside Vincent Racaniello, makes the case that the largest risk isn't the mink themselves but what their infection implies: each new mammalian host is another roll of the dice toward human adaptation. The same episode contrasts this with Marburg virus, where a Cell Reports study explains why the virus is harmless in its bat reservoir but lethal in people — bats mount a narrow, restrained antiviral response while humans trigger broad inflammatory damage. The report also covers rising rabies exposure costs, why measles is unlikely to escape its vaccine, and a global survey showing trust in science fell from 80% in 2020 to 72% in 2025. For investors, the through-line is that underfunded surveillance and eroding public trust are themselves systemic risks in any pandemic-exposed portfolio. Key Elements H5N1USDAUtah mink farmB3.13 genotypeMarburg virusCell ReportsGustavo PalaciosAlvin CrespoThomas PeacockWellcome Global MonitorCDCEgyptian rousette batPLOS ONEPirbright Institute Something is moving H5N1 between American farms, and the people whose job it is to track it have stopped taking samples. In August 2026, USDA inspectors collected swabs from captive mink at a farm in Salt Lake County, Utah. The virus they found was not the bird flu that arrives each season with wild waterfowl. Genetically, all ten whole-genome sequences the team analyzed belonged to B3.13 — the cattle-adapted lineage that first appeared in Texas dairy herds in March 2024 — according to a preprint discussed by virologists Rich Condit and Alan Dove on the podcast This Week in Virology. The mink outbreak matters because of what it tells us about the virus's staying power. "The longer the virus spends in mammals, the better the chances we think that it will be able to adapt to humans and establish human-to-human spread," Dove said. Cattle and mink are two very different mammals. A virus that can jump between them is a virus testing new furniture in the house. A six-week head start, and one mutation to watch The epidemiological timeline is the story here. Genetic analysis places the most recent common ancestor of the Utah mink cluster at July 4, 2026. The samples weren't collected until August 7, and detection was confirmed ten days later. That means the virus circulated in the mink sheds for roughly six weeks before anyone looked. By September 29, the outbreak was reportedly still ongoing — and no further samples had been taken after that first batch. The sequences point to a single introduction, followed by diversification. Ten genomes, one cluster, all nested inside the 2026 B3.13 cattle group alongside strains from Idaho, Texas, and Utah dairy herds. One shared mutation, PB1 K388R, appears in every mink virus — a change rarely seen in wild birds but previously documented in Spanish mink infected in October 2022. The viruses also carry PB2 M631L, a cattle mutation known to boost replication in mammals. Finding Detail Genotype B3.13, the cattle-adapted clade first seen in Texas in March 2024 Cluster structure Single introduction, then diversification — all 10 sequences in one cluster Estimated entry into mink July 4, 2026 (most recent common ancestor) Detection August 17, 2026; samples from August 7 Shared mutation PB1 K388R in all 10 mink viruses; previously seen in Spanish mink, October 2022 Pre-existing cattle mutations PB2 M631L (increases replication in mammals); neuraminidase changes linked to earlier mink outbreaks Closest relatives Texas cattle strains sampled May 2026, with a three-month gap How the virus traveled from Texas cattle to Utah mink is unknown — and the authors are candid about it. Their candidate list: shared workers, vehicles or equipment moving between farms, or feed. Mink are carnivores, often fed poultry products that travel long distances across state lines. Farm labor is precarious and mobile. "Our inability to pinpoint the source of the Utah outbreak points to large surveillance gaps for highly pathogenic avian influenza in US livestock," the authors wrote, as read by host Vincent Racaniello. The gaps run deeper than one farm. H5N1 has been detected in dairy cattle in Kansas and Washington, but no sequences exist for those herds, so their relationship to the Utah cluster is unknown. There may be unidentified infected herds near the mink farm. Utah and Idaho are two of the largest mink-producing states, and both have continuing B3.13 circulation in cattle — a coincidence the preprint's authors treat as a risk factor. The policy problem is the real outbreak Here is where the science meets the politics, and where the hosts don't hold back. By the preprint's account, neither the state nor the USDA ordered culling or monitored exposed farm workers. The USDA told the state veterinarian there was no mechanism to authorize vaccination or to compensate the producer for mink losses. Alleged fur-industry pressure has discouraged regulation. Federal interstate testing requirements apply to lactating dairy cattle, yet the virus keeps shuttling between Idaho, Texas, and Utah. Dove pushes back on the easy explanation that a different administration would simply test more. He argues the USDA has a structural conflict baked into its mission — promoting agriculture on one side, protecting public health on the other — and that farm margins are razor thin. Any intervention carries real cost and real political resistance. Poultry culling happens because export markets demand it. There is no comparable export pressure for mink pelts: 80% of the farmed mink market is in China, which means US producers have almost no domestic demand to protect — and little industry-side incentive to fund surveillance or accept a cull. Why mink in particular? "Mink could be a vessel for reassortment between human and avian strains," Condit said. Farmed mink are one of the few settings where a human-adapted influenza virus might meet a bird-adapted one inside the same animal. The preprint's own open questions read like a to-do list nobody has picked up: will the virus spread to other mink farms (SARS-CoV-2 spread across six Utah mink farms in 2020, possibly via free-roaming cats), is it still adapting to mink, and why haven't wild birds seeded North American mink outbreaks the way gulls did in Spain and Finland? For perspective on severity, consider the baseline. Avian-origin H5N1 kills up to half the people it infects. The cattle-linked B genotype has produced mostly mild human cases; the D genotype has been more severe. "When humans get avian H5N1 from birds it has a high fatality, right up to 50%," Racaniello said. "The cases associated with the B genotype from cows have been mild." The mink viruses belong to the cattle world. That is reassuring today. The question is what six more weeks — or six more months — of unchecked mammalian circulation does to that picture. Why Marburg kills us and leaves bats alone The second paper in the episode answers a question that sounds like trivia but isn't: how does a virus that is lethal to humans live peacefully inside a bat? A study in Cell Reports, led by Gustavo Palacios with co-first authors André Philippe Pastor and Shruti Sherath, compared Egyptian rousette bat cells and human cells to find out. The design was simple: hit fibroblasts with poly(I:C), a synthetic stand-in for double-stranded viral RNA, for four hours, then sequence the RNA and compare what each species switched on. Four functional gene classes emerged — human genes sitting low at baseline, bat genes sitting low at baseline, genes the bats induced strongly, and genes the humans induced strongly. The pattern is the point. Bat cells switched on a tight set of antiviral effectors such as ZBP1 and held inflammatory signals like chemokines and IL-6 in check. Human cells lit up a broad inflammatory and cytokine program plus Toll-like receptors and RIG-I pathway components, along with genes tied to necroptosis and apoptosis. The authors describe the bat response as "robust yet restrained" and the human one as "indiscriminate inflammatory amplification" — the mechanism they suspect drives the cytokine storm that makes Marburg disease so destructive. The hosts repeatedly invoke a warning from a colleague: "if you've seen one bat, you've seen one bat." Bat immunology varies wildly across species — some have a contracted type I interferon locus with constitutive interferon alpha, others have expanded interferon omega genes, some lack interferon alpha entirely. Interferon epsilon gets its own section in the paper: rousette intestinal organoids and airway cultures show elevated constitutive epsilon and interferon-stimulated genes, and a prior study concluded epsilon production drives bat immune resilience. In rousettes, epsilon is highest in brain, ovaries, and liver, absent from most immune tissues, and unchanged after infection — largely mirroring human biology. Condit raises the main methodological caveat, and it's worth hearing because it's how real science gets read. These are dermal fibroblasts — not immune cells, not endothelial cells, and not the epithelial frontline where infection starts. He checked the cell bank records and found the cells are around passage 14, subcultured enough to be effectively pure fibroblasts rather than the primary cells the paper sometimes calls them. His verdict is generous: this is a resource paper, and a useful one. It hands other labs a tractable system and a gene list to test — and it validates against real infection data from Marburg-infected rousette spleens rather than standing alone. Rabies shots cost $11,000 to $14,000 — and exposures are up 17% A CDC health alert reports a nationwide rise in reported human rabies exposures and post-exposure treatment. From July through August 2026, the CDC fielded 17% more rabies-related inquiries than in the same period of 2025, including mass exposure events involving more than one person. A typical post-exposure course runs $11,000 to $14,000. The hosts use a listener question — from two Minnesota veterinarians whose clients refuse to vaccinate their pets — to walk through the economics. If a rabid pet bites someone, the owner faces serious financial liability, not just the prophylaxis bill. Dog rabies vaccination is legally required in every state the hosts have lived in, with a tag as proof; unvaccinated, unlicensed dogs can be confiscated and euthanized. The human vaccine is inactivated virus; the pet vaccine is a poxvirus-vectored rabies glycoprotein, commonly canarypox, given annually or every other year. The old painful abdominal series is gone. Condit's bottom line: the disease is effectively 100% fatal once symptoms appear, so there is no reason to gamble. And the gamble isn't only the owner's — it's the neighbor who gets bitten. Measles won't escape the vaccine, so don't run the experiment A listener asks whether rampant measles in under-vaccinated populations could select a variant that defeats the current vaccine. The short answer is probably not, and the reasoning is elegant. Measles is monotypic — one strain worldwide, as far as anyone knows — and the vaccine has stayed effective for roughly six decades. People who survived natural infection were immune for life, which is what made a vaccine plausible in the first place. The explanation is fitness cost. Racaniello recalls a paper in which the measles glycoprotein was mutated up and down: some regions tolerate change, others cannot — analogous to an aircraft returning from battle with damage concentrated where it matters least. Influenza's hemagglutinin has more room to tinker around the active site, and its segmented genome plus avian reservoir make antigenic variation a different problem entirely. Dove offers a fitness-landscape analogy: adaptation is like climbing out of a gravity well, and measles sits in a deep one. The hosts note they asked an AI, which agreed there are no known antibody-escape mutants, then joked it may simply have mined their own conversation. Condit's verdict: a damn interesting question, and still not a good experiment to run. Trust in science fell to 72% — and it tracks inequality The Wellcome Global Monitor 2026, "Trust in Science Under Pressure," draws on 140,000 adults across 140 countries — the largest study of public trust in science. Three findings stand out: trust has fallen since the COVID-19 pandemic; trust is associated with stronger, more equal societies; and trust is higher among people who see science as relevant and beneficial to them. Measure 2018 2020 2025 Report some or a lot of trust in science 77% 80% 72% Trust in science has fallen more than trust in other institutions, and low-trust groups are further apart from everyone else in their attitudes. The country-level picture is stark: Finland tops the chart with 66% high trust and 76% saying science benefits most people; Somalia sits at the bottom with 9%, alongside Congo, Ecuador, Albania, and Peru. The US lands at 53% high trust and 57% on benefit — higher than the hosts expected. Racaniello attributes part of the decline to visible anti-science actors, particularly the anti-vaccine movement, and part to a perception that public health overreached during COVID. Dove counters that people at the bottom of the chart are dealing with more immediate dangers, and that much of what is bad in their lives also comes from science. The relevance to everything above is direct. Surveillance, vaccination, and culling are all public acts. They require a public that trusts the institutions asking. Health care ranks last at 4% Racaniello opens the political segment with a New York Times poll by Echelon Insights of voters who backed Trump in 2024 and no longer support him. Cost of living tops the list at 42%. Immigration sits at 5%, democracy at 6%, and health care dead last at 4%. His argument: health care is being trashed — the CDC, infectious disease tracking, science funding — and there is a measles outbreak that should not exist. Dove's reading is that the question forced a single biggest issue, and someone three months behind on rent will name rent. The exchange turns to constitutional safeguards. Dove notes the framers built checks against a demagogue but did not anticipate an entire party going along; Condit notes they assumed rational, well-informed negotiators. Dove plugs the PBS NewsHour for putting people of diverse views into actual discussion, and argues that well-informed coverage tilts left because it tries to be reality-based. Four dollars of cardboard, four days instead of two weeks The episode ends somewhere unexpectedly practical. Condit's pick, passed along by his wife: a PLOS ONE randomized controlled trial titled "The effect of a hiding box on stress levels and body weight in Dutch shelter cats." Twenty-three cats newly arrived at a shelter were assigned a cardboard box or not. Body weight was unaffected — all cats lose weight while adjusting. But on a structured stress rubric covering posture and ear position, cats with boxes adapted in about four days versus roughly two weeks without. The mechanism is concealment and security. Condit connects it to his own cat's boarding experience at a purpose-built cattery: a large room with floor-to-ceiling mesh enclosures, cat trees, windows, and daily photos. The first two days of photos reproducibly showed the cat hiding in a box in the cat tree; after that he came out to play. Dove notes the behavior isn't domestic-cat-specific — his local zoo's bobcats curl up in Amazon boxes. Dove's pick is Edward Gorey's Fantod Pack, a tarot-style deck of about 20 cards in the artist's dark Victorian line style, timed for Halloween and the gift season. Racaniello's pick is the Wellcome Global Monitor itself. A listener recommends Michael Lewis's Who Is Government? and Blockers, the latter covering DOGE, Project 2025, and the removal of experienced civil servants, including a chapter on Francis Collins trying to determine who forced him out. What to watch The unresolved tension is between what the sequences show and what the surveillance system can see. The Utah mink cluster has a single introduction, a known date, and a shared mutation — PB1 K388R — that has now appeared in mammals at least twice. But the intermediate host, the feed source, and any nearby infected herds remain unidentified, and no samples have been taken since the first week of August. The same blind spot applies to cattle in Kansas and Washington, where H5N1 has been detected but never sequenced. For anyone thinking about risk rather than just reading about it, the message is uncomfortable: the systems that would catch a pandemic early are being switched off, not because the science failed but because the funding, compensation, and political will behind them failed. The Wellcome data suggest the public whose support those systems depend on is losing confidence at the same time. And the one policy lever that might change the mink outbreak's trajectory — paying producers to cull — has been ruled out as unavailable. The bat paper's contribution is more durable: a validated, tractable cell system and a gene list other labs can now test across species. That is exactly the kind of work that depends on stable research funding — which, in this episode's telling,...

美国农业部确认印第安纳州奶牛群感染 H5N1 禽流感 - 农场和乳制品

USDA confirms H5N1 bird flu in Indiana dairy herd - Farm and Dairy
新闻简报据 Farm and Dairy 于 2026 年 11 月 5 日报道,美国农业部确认印第安纳州的一个奶牛群中存在 H5N1 禽流感。这一确认凸显了对农业环境中 禽流感 的持续关注。文章指出,该病毒已在牛群中被发现,但没有提供有关受影响动物数量、死亡人数或 暴发 范围的具体细节。该报告还强调了监测和管理此类 暴发 以保护牲畜和人类健康的重要性。该消息是通过官方渠道确认的,文章暗示可能会采取进一步行动来遏制病毒的传播。然而,根据提供的证据,回应的细节和 暴发 的当前状态仍不清楚。

USDA confirms H5N1 bird flu in Indiana dairy herd - Farm and Dairy

News Brief
The USDA confirmed the presence of H5N1 bird flu in a dairy herd in Indiana, as reported by Farm and Dairy on November 5, 2026. This confirmation highlights the ongoing concern about avian influenza in agricultural settings. The article notes that the virus has been identified in the herd, but it does not provide specific details on the number of affected animals, deaths, or the extent of the outbreak. The report also emphasizes the importance of monitoring and managing such outbreaks to protect both livestock and human health. The confirmation was made through official channels, and the article suggests that further actions may be taken to contain the spread of the virus. However, the details of the response and the current status of the outbreak remain unclear based on the supplied evidence.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

华盛顿州居民因接触受感染的牛而死于禽流感后该怎么办 - ABC7 New York - ABC7 New York

What to know after Washington state resident dies of bird flu after being exposed to infected cattle - ABC7 New York - ABC7 New York
新闻简报华盛顿州一名居民在接触受感染的牛后死于禽流感,卫生官员认为这是今年首例此类死亡。该人有潜在的健康问题,因重病住院,位于华盛顿中南部靠近俄勒冈州的亚基马县。该病例是更广泛趋势的一部分,华盛顿今年报告了三起禽流感病例,全部与奶牛场工作或接触的人有关。公共卫生专家强调,虽然普通人群面临的风险仍然很低,但人们对与季节性流感可能重叠的担忧有些轻微,季节性流感已经开始在该地区传播。专家警告称,该病毒可能会发生变异并获得在人与人之间传播的能力,但目前尚无证据表明存在人传人的传播。这一死亡事件提醒人们考虑感染季节性流感 疫苗,特别是在流感活动加剧且预计将持续整个冬季的情况下。
查看新闻五要素
时间
据卫生官员报道,这名华盛顿州居民于 2026 年 11 月死亡。事件日期没有明确说明,但死亡发生在当前流感季节,该州流感活动加剧。
地点与对象
死者是华盛顿州亚基马县的居民,该县位于华盛顿中南部,与俄勒冈州接壤。受影响的人群包括在奶牛场工作或接触受感染奶牛的人。
事件
华盛顿州一名居民在接触受感染的牛后死于禽流感。据当地卫生官员报道,这被认为是今年首例禽流感死亡病例。
规模、影响与风险
这是华盛顿州今年第三起禽流感病例,全部与接触受感染奶牛的人有关。该人有潜在的健康状况。公共卫生专家指出,公众面临的风险较低,但随着季节性流感开始传播,人们对人类禽流感病例与季节性流感重叠的担忧有些轻微。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生官员已获知禽流感死亡事件,并正在监测事态发展。Andrew Pekosz 和 William Schaffner 等专家对该病毒的持续存在以及季节性流感 疫苗接种 的重要性发表了评论。CDC 提供了 H5N1 病毒的彩色电子显微镜图像,但没有提及具体的测试或隔离细节。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

What to know after Washington state resident dies of bird flu after being exposed to infected cattle - ABC7 New York - ABC7 New York

News Brief
A Washington state resident has died from bird flu after being exposed to infected cattle, marking what health officials believe is the first such death of the year. The individual, who had underlying health conditions, was hospitalized with severe illness in Yakima County, which is located in south central Washington near Oregon. This case is part of a broader trend, as Washington has reported three bird flu cases this year, all linked to people working on or in contact with dairy farms. Public health specialists emphasize that while the risk to the general population remains low, there is a mild concern about potential overlap with seasonal influenza, which has already started to spread in the region. Experts warn that the virus could mutate and gain the ability to spread between humans, but currently, there is no evidence of person-to-person transmission. The death serves as a reminder for people to consider getting their seasonal influenza vaccine, especially as flu activity is elevated and expected to continue through winter.
Original source text (English)
24/7 LiveNew York CityNew JerseyLong IslandNorthern SuburbsConnecticut 71° Bird Flu Washington state resident dies of bird flu after being exposed to infected cattle | What to know The person who died was exposed to infected cattle, officials said. Watch ABC News live news stream and get 24/7 latest, breaking news coverage, and live video. ByMary Kekatos Tuesday, November 3, 2026 5:02PM Share Washington state health officials this week reported what is believed to be the first bird flu death of the year. The person who died was a resident of Yakima County, in south central Washington on the border with Oregon, who was hospitalized with "severe illness," authorities noted. Officials said the person contracted bird flu, also known as avian influenza, following exposure to infected cattle. They had underlying health conditions, according to the Yakima Health District. This colorized electron microscope image shows avian influenza A H5N1 virus particles (yellow), grown in Madin-Darby Canine Kidney (MDCK) epithelial cells (blue). (CDC/NIAID via AP, File) Public health specialists said the death is a reminder that bird flu is still present and circulating. "While we haven't heard much about it, maybe in the last year or so, the virus has not gone away because there have been periodic detections of this [bird flu] in dairy cattle," Andrew Pekosz, professor and vice chair of the W. Harry Feinstone department of molecular microbiology and immunology at Johns Hopkins Bloomberg School of Public Health, told ABC News. According to Pekosz, "the sequence of the virus" shows that it has remained active. "It's sort of the same virus that has been around for a couple of years now," he said. "So, this all indicates that the virus continues to spread in dairy cows." Washington state has recorded three cases of bird flu so far this year, all associated with people who either work at dairy farms or were in contact with infected dairy cows, according to local reports. Top Stories Bronx vigil honors murdered 15-year-old student, Javits Frimpong 2 hours ago Cascon Cheesecake in Queens robbed of $40,000 in overnight theft Family seeks answers after 24-year-old found dead in duffel bag in Brooklyn 2 hours ago Pekosz said he agrees with local health officials that the risk to the general public is low and that most people do not need to be concerned. However, he said the death is a reminder to public health officials to be more aware of bird flu viruses. He added that there is also mild concern about bird flu cases in humans being confirmed as seasonal influenza start to spread, arriving early in several parts of the country. Data from the Washington State Department of Health shows that influenza activity in the state is elevated and increasing. Experts have previously said recombination of bird flu and seasonal influenza is unlikely because bird flu is not yet showing evidence of person-to-person transmission. "We need to keep our eye on that because there still is a possibility that this virus could mutate, pick up the genetic capacity to spread readily from human to human," Dr. William Schaffner, a professor of preventive medicine at Vanderbilt University Medical Center in Nashville, told ABC News. "It can't do that at the present time, although it can in ... people with chronic illnesses, such as this person in the state of Washington who unfortunately died of a bird flu illness." Schaffner and Pekosz said this is a reminder for people to get their seasonal influenza vaccine if they have not done so already, and that it's not too late. "Influenza started early in many parts of the country, including my own," Schaffner said. "But remember, flu lasts throughout December into January and into February and even beyond. So, there's plenty of winter to come, during which you want to have as much protection against severe disease that the vaccine can provide." B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

华盛顿州居民感染禽流感后死于“重病” - 卫报

Washington state resident dies from ‘severe illness’ after contracting bird flu - The Guardian
新闻简报华盛顿州一名居民感染禽流感后死亡,这是美国今年首例因禽流感并发症死亡的病例。该人有潜在的健康问题,在亚基马县接触过受感染的奶牛,随后住院。当地卫生官员与华盛顿州卫生部合作,确认了该县第三例人感染禽流感病例。虽然公众面临的风险仍然很低,但乳制品工人和长期接触受感染动物的人面临的风险更大。美国疾病控制和预防中心表示,人与人之间的禽流感 传播 极为罕见,在美国尚未有记录。官员们正在进行接触者追踪,以监测潜在暴露者的症状。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该患者于 2026 年 11 月 1 日(星期日)住院治疗并死亡。2025 年 1 月报道了美国首例因禽流感并发症死亡的病例。
地点与对象
该事件发生在华盛顿州亚基马县,涉及一名患有基础健康问题并接触受感染奶牛的居民。
事件
一名华盛顿州居民因接触 禽流感 A H5N1 病毒而患重病住院治疗后死亡。这是美国今年首例因禽流感并发症死亡的病例。
规模、影响与风险
自 10 月初以来,亚基马县已报告 33 个奶牛群的 高致病性禽流感 检测呈阳性。该人是该县第三例确诊的人类病例。公众面临的风险仍然很低,但乳制品工人和与受感染动物密切接触的人面临更高的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生官员正在监测人类病例并确定可能与死者有过密切接触的个人。疾病预防控制中心已确认美国没有已知的人与人之间传播禽流感的情况。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Washington state resident dies from ‘severe illness’ after contracting bird flu - The Guardian

News Brief
A Washington state resident has died after contracting bird flu, marking the first fatality in the US this year from complications of the disease. The individual, who had underlying health conditions, was exposed to infected dairy cattle in Yakima county and subsequently hospitalized. Local health officials, in collaboration with the Washington state department of health, confirmed the third human case of bird flu in the county. While the risk to the general public remains low, dairy workers and those with prolonged contact with infected animals are at greater risk. The CDC has stated that human-to-human transmission of bird flu is extremely rare and has not been documented in the US. Officials are conducting contact tracing to monitor for symptoms in potentially exposed individuals.
Original source text (English)

Photograph: National Institute of Allergy and Infectious Diseases/AP View image in fullscreen A colorized electron microscope image shows avian influenza A H5N1 virus particles (yellow), grown in epithelial cells (blue). Photograph: National Institute of Allergy and Infectious Diseases/AP Washington state resident dies from ‘severe illness’ after contracting bird flu Individual, who also had underlying conditions, was hospitalized after being exposed to avian influenza A Washington state resident has died after being hospitalized with “severe illness” after contracting bird flu, local health officials said on Sunday. The individual was exposed to avian influenza, also known as bird flu, at a dairy with infected cattle in Yakima county, according to a news release. The person was subsequently hospitalized with severe illness and died on Sunday. Health officials said that the individual, whose identity had not been released, had underlying health conditions. “The Yakima health district offers its heartfelt condolences to the individual’s family, friends, and loved ones during this difficult time,” the district said in a statement. This is the first person in the United States to die from complications from bird flu this year, according to the Associated Press. It comes as officials in Yakima county have reported that since early October, 33 dairy herds have tested positive for “highly pathogenic avian influenza”. ‘It’s clear they are under-vaccinated’: Amish under spotlight amid Pennsylvania measles outbreak Read more The Yakima health district has said that it, in coordination with the Washington state department of health, is monitoring human cases of bird flu in Yakima county. So far, the district has recorded three confirmed human cases in the county. In Sunday’s statement, the health district announced the county’s third confirmed human case of bird flu. The individual was “exposed to infected dairy cattle at a Yakima county dairy”, but has not experienced severe illness or required hospitalization. Officials said they are working with public health partners to identify and contact any individuals “who may have been in close contact with the person to monitor for symptoms”. County officials have stressed that “the risk of avian influenza infection to the general public remains low” and that “people at greater risk include dairy workers and others who have close or prolonged contact with infected animals or environments contaminated with the virus”. Human-to-human transmission of bird flu is “extremely rare and has never been documented in the United States”, they said. The US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) has recorded 72 human cases of bird flu across the country since 2024, according to agency data. The CDC has maintained that there is no known person-to-person spread and that the risk to the general public remains low. The CDC announced the first death in the US due to complications from bird flu in January 2025. Event

Scale, impact and risk
Yakima county has reported 33 dairy herds testing positive for highly pathogenic avian influenza since early October. The individual was the third confirmed human case in the county. The risk to the general public remains low, but dairy workers and those with close contact with infected animals are at higher risk.
Response, status and uncertainty
Health officials are monitoring human cases and identifying individuals who may have been in close contact with the deceased. The CDC has confirmed no known person-to-person spread of bird flu in the US.

亚基马县禽流感死亡病例为美国今年首例,确认第三例与乳制品有关的人类病例 - inkl

Yakima County Bird Flu Death Is First in U.S. This Year as Third Dairy-Linked Human Case Is Confirmed - inkl
新闻简报美国华盛顿州亚基马县的一名居民在接触受感染的奶牛后死于 H5N1 禽流感,这是 2026 年美国首例禽流感死亡病例,也是自 2024 年在奶牛中发现该病毒以来的第三例死亡病例。亚基马卫生区和疾病预防控制中心证实了该病例,指出该人有基础健康状况,并因病情严重而入院治疗。这起死亡事件是影响华盛顿州 40 个奶牛群的更广泛的 暴发 事件的一部分,但接受检测的暴露农场工人的数量仍未报告。该事件发生于2026年11月3日,目前情况正在调查中。该病毒被归类为 H5N1,高致病性禽流感 亚型,其在牛中的传播引起了人们对潜在人类 传播 的担忧。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 11 月 3 日报道了亚基马县禽流感死亡事件,该事件发生在正在进行的 2026 年 暴发 背景下。第三例与乳制品相关的人类病例在同一时间得到确认。
地点与对象
该事件发生在美国华盛顿州亚基马县,一名县居民接触了受感染的奶牛。受影响的人群包括可能通过接触受感染动物而接触到病毒的个人。
事件
亚基马卫生区和疾病预防控制中心证实,一名亚基马县居民在接触受感染的奶牛后死于 H5N1 禽流感。这是 2026 年美国首例禽流感死亡病例,也是 2024 年以来第三例与奶牛相关的人类病例。
规模、影响与风险
这是美国今年首例因禽流感死亡的病例,也是自2024年在奶牛中发现该病毒以来的第三例。美国疾病控制与预防中心确认该病毒为H5N1,该人有基础健康状况,因病情严重而入院治疗。据报道,病毒在华盛顿州的奶牛中传播,已有 40 头奶牛受到感染。
应对、状态与不确定性
亚基马卫生区和疾病预防控制中心证实了这一病例和死亡。然而,接受测试的暴露农场工人人数仍未报告,这表明 暴发 的整个范围存在一些不确定性。目前情况正在调查中,并正在努力监测病毒的传播。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Yakima County Bird Flu Death Is First in U.S. This Year as Third Dairy-Linked Human Case Is Confirmed - inkl

News Brief
A Yakima County resident in Washington, United States, has died from H5N1 bird flu after exposure to infected dairy cattle, marking the first U.S. bird flu death of 2026 and the third since the virus was found in dairy cattle in 2024. The Yakima Health District and CDC confirmed the case, noting the individual had underlying health conditions and was hospitalized with severe illness. This death is part of a broader outbreak affecting 40 dairy herds in Washington state, though the number of exposed farmworkers tested remains unreported. The event occurred on November 3, 2026, and the situation is under investigation. The virus is classified as H5N1, a highly pathogenic avian influenza subtype, and its spread among cattle has raised concerns about potential human transmission.
Original source text (English)

PBS Newshour A Yakima County resident who caught H5N1 bird flu after exposure to infected dairy cattle has died, the Yakima Health District said Nov. 1. The person had underlying health conditions and had been hospitalized with severe illness, health officials said, and the CDC confirmed the virus as H5N1, PBS NewsHour reported. It is the first U.S. bird flu death of 2026 and the third since the virus was found in dairy cattle in 2024. The health district also confirmed a third human case in a county resident exposed to infected cattle at a local dairy. That person has not been severely ill or hospitalized. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Third U.S. Bird Flu Death Since Virus Spread to Dairy Cattle - JournalArta

News Brief
A Yakima County resident in Washington, U.S., has died after contracting H5N1 bird flu following exposure to infected dairy cattle, marking the third U.S. death linked to the virus since it began spreading among livestock. The individual, who had underlying health conditions, was hospitalized and died on Sunday, November 6, 2026. This death is the first in the U.S. from bird flu complications in 2026. Health officials have stated that the risk to the general public remains low, though the virus can cause serious illness in people with existing health problems. The CDC confirmed the virus as H5N1, and the deaths and new infections are being monitored as the virus continues to be detected in cattle. Officials did not release the person’s name, and the outbreak is part of a broader trend of H5N1 cases in the U.S. since 2024, with over 70 human cases recorded.
Original source text (English)

H5N1 Hits Indiana Dairy Farms: 2 Newton County Herds Quarantined as Statewide Restrictions Take Effect

News Brief
H5N1 has been confirmed in two dairy herds in Newton County, Indiana, triggering mandatory movement restrictions and a 10-km surveillance zone. The Indiana State Board of Animal Health is implementing weekly testing and a voluntary herd status program to manage the outbreak. No food safety threat to the public is reported, but pet owners are advised against feeding uncooked diets or raw milk to household animals. Biosecurity measures are emphasized to prevent further spread.
Original source text (English)

HAT Radio Stations Contact Us About HAT Advertise with HAT Home News Markets YOUR Purdue Extension! Indiana Ag Podcasts Indiana Farm Forecast Listen to HAT The All New HAT App! HAT Radio Stations Contact Us About HAT Advertise with HAT H5N1 Hits Indiana Dairy Farms: 2 Newton County Herds Quarantined as Statewide Restrictions Take Effect by C.J. Miller November 2, 2026 Photo: C.J. Miller / Hoosier Ag Today. Hoosier dairy producers are on high alert following the confirmation of the H5N1 influenza virus in two operationally connected dairy herds in Newton County in northwestern Indiana. The detection officially strips Indiana of its “unaffected state” status and triggers immediate mandatory movement restrictions, farm quarantines, and a 10-kilometer surveillance buffer zone near the affected farms. The initial detection did not originate from clinical signs on the farm, but through routine National Milk Testing Strategy (NMTS) bulk-tank surveillance at an out-of-state dairy processing facility. After the virus was flagged in a holding silo receiving Indiana milk, the Indiana State Board of Animal Health (BOAH) collected follow-up samples at the source farms. The tests were confirmed positive by the Indiana Animal Disease Diagnostic Laboratory (ADDL) and verified by the USDA National Veterinary Services Laboratories (NVSL) in Ames, Iowa. When pressed on where the processing facility is located, BOAH officials declined to identify the specific state of origin, stating that state health officials and veterinarians in that jurisdiction are still actively investigating epidemiological links and contacting partners before details are made public. For Indiana’s 660 dairy operations—which together produce more than 4.51 billion pounds of milk annually—the regulatory landscape has immediately shifted. Indiana is now the 21st state in the U.S. where a positive case of H5N1 has been detected in dairy cattle. Mandatory Interstate Testing & Herd Status Options Under USDA federal orders, any lactating dairy cow or dairy-beef cross leaving Indiana must now test negative for H5N1 prior to interstate movement. Private herd veterinarians must collect individual animal samples for testing through the ADDL or accredited diagnostic facilities before shipment paperwork can be cleared. To bypass individual animal testing delays and reduce paperwork for high-frequency shippers, BOAH is urging regular interstate exporters to enroll in the USDA Dairy Herd Status Program. “Under a normal movement, every individual animal needs to be tested before that lactating dairy cow can move across state lines,” explained Denise Derrer Spears, Public Information Officer for BOAH. “If the herd is tested on a weekly basis, that establishes a status for all the animals in that herd, and therefore they can just make the movement as needed.” Producers interested in enrolling in the voluntary program should contact BOAH at animalhealth@boah.in.gov or work directly with their herd veterinarian. Quarantine Protocol & The 10-km Surveillance Zone For the two infected dairies in Newton County, strict quarantine orders are in effect for all lactating cattle. Under BOAH’s emergency response plan, farm-level testing will continue weekly until the herds achieve two consecutive negative bulk-tank tests, at which point the quarantine will be lifted. The impact extends well beyond the two affected operations. BOAH has established an active 10-kilometer (6.2-mile) Surveillance Zone around the infected sites. All Grade A dairy farms and commercial poultry facilities located within this 10-km perimeter are subject to mandatory community spread verification. Surveillance testing within the zone will be conducted every 28 days until the quarantined farms complete their clearance protocols. To assist with wildlife vectors, USDA Wildlife Services and the Indiana Department of Natural Resources (DNR) are conducting active wild bird surveillance in and around the Newton County surveillance perimeter. Wild waterfowl were identified as the primary catalyst when H5N1 first crossed into U.S. dairy cattle in Texas back in March 2024. Biosecurity Focus: Transmission Routes Between Operations Epidemiological investigations into H5N1 outbreaks across the Midwest—including USDA findings from Michigan’s 2024 dairy event—reveal that disease spread between dairies is rarely driven by live animal additions. Instead, transmission occurs through indirect operational links: shared personnel, contract haulers, deadstock pickup, feed deliveries, and unpasteurized milk transport. Because the two Newton County herds were described as operationally connected, BOAH is warning neighboring farms and connected operations to audit every daily off-farm traffic point. “Really, what it gets down to when it comes to biosecurity, the basics are really, really important: clean hands, clean feet, clean vehicles,” Spears emphasized. “Cleaning your hands and feet before you get in and out of a truck, a tractor, or any type of vehicle on and off of those farms is really important, especially before going to the next farm.” Spears cautioned producers against viewing biosecurity as a seasonal task. “Biosecurity is really important, and that’s 365 days a year,” Spears said. “You can’t just think, ‘Oh, it’s high-path flu season,’ and just do it now. It can happen to anybody, anytime, and it just really emphasizes—regardless of the species or the type of operation—clean hands, clean feet, clean equipment.” Because the H5N1 virus is heavily concentrated in raw, unpasteurized milk, BOAH stresses that equipment, tires, and footwear exposed to parlor wash water or raw milk must be thoroughly disinfected before leaving any facility. Consumer Confidence & Herd Monitoring State health and agricultural officials reiterate that the discovery presents no food safety threat to the public. Commercial pasteurization effectively inactivates the H5N1 virus, and pasteurized milk, eggs, beef, and poultry remain completely safe for consumers. However, pet owners are advised against feeding uncooked diets or raw milk to household animals, as domestic cats are highly susceptible to severe H5N1 clinical illness. Producers across Indiana should closely monitor cattle for hallmark symptoms, including: Sudden drop in herd-wide milk production Tacky, thickened, or colostrum-like milk consistency Decreased feed intake and rumen motility Diarrhea, lethargy, dehydration, and fever If clinical signs appear, cattle owners should isolate affected animals and contact their herd veterinarian immediately. For real-time updates on Indiana’s H5N1 dashboard, surveillance status, and e-newsletter alerts, visit the Indiana State Board of Animal Health website at in.gov/boah. Original title (Chinese)
美国华盛顿州出现禽流感死亡病例,我们需要对此感到恐慌吗? |#科学资讯# #医学# #H5N1禽流感#
News Brief
A resident in Washington State, USA, with a pre-existing health condition, died after contracting highly pathogenic avian influenza from an infected cow. This is the third reported case of human infection with the H5N1 virus in the U.S. since 2024. Public health officials are investigating the transmission chain and assessing the risk of community spread. Scientists are monitoring genetic changes in the virus to understand its potential for further adaptation.
Original source text (Chinese)
新闻 体育 汽车 房产 旅游 教育 时尚 科技 财经 娱乐 美国华盛顿州出现禽流感死亡病例,我们需要对此感到恐慌吗? |#科学资讯# #医学# #H5N1禽流感# 2026-11-03 06:02 来源: 常识技能百宝箱 近期,美国华盛顿州卫生部门报告了一起令人关注的公共卫生事件:一名患有基础疾病的居民在接触了受感染的奶牛后,不幸感染了高致病性禽流感(Avian Influenza),并最终导致死亡。这是自2024年以来,美国境内记录的第三例与该病毒相关的死亡病例。 什么是H5N1禽流感? 这次事件的核心对象是H5N1型禽流感病毒。从生物学角度看,这是一种主要在鸟类中传播的病毒,但近年来,它展现出了跨物种传播的能力,开始在部分哺乳动物(如奶牛)中出现感染迹象。约翰斯·霍普金斯大学(Johns Hopkins University)彭博公共卫生学院的安德鲁·佩科斯(Andrew Pekosz)教授指出,这类病毒的跨物种溢出效应是当前公共卫生领域监测的重点。 为什么这起病例值得关注? 在华盛顿州近期发现的三例人类感染病例中,这起死亡事件显得尤为突出。科学家们关注的重点在于,病毒是否在哺乳动物之间发生了变异,从而增强了对人类的潜在威胁。目前,卫生部门正在对相关病例进行流行病学调查,以确定病毒的具体传播链条,并评估是否存在社区传播的风险。 我们需要保持怎样的科学态度? 面对此类新闻,公众容易产生焦虑,但我们需要保持冷静的科学判断。首先,目前所有的证据都指向该患者是在接触受感染奶牛的过程中被传染的,这属于典型的“人畜共患”路径,而非人际间的广泛传播。其次,该患者本身患有基础健康状况,这在医学上是影响病毒感染后果的重要变量,不能简单地将个案的严重性等同于病毒对普通健康人群的普遍威胁。 还有哪些不确定性? 现阶段的证据显示,虽然H5N1病毒在农场动物中的存在是一个明确的科学事实,但它是否会进一步演化出更易在人与人之间传播的特征,目前尚无定论。科研人员正在密切监测病毒的基因序列变化,以寻找任何可能增强其致病性或传播力的突变。在更多深入的分子微生物学研究结果出炉之前,任何过度解读或恐慌都是没有必要的。 总结来说,公共卫生监测的意义在于防患于未然。我们应当关注官方发布的卫生建议,避免接触病死动物,并保持良好的个人卫生习惯。科学的应对之道,永远是基于严谨的数据监测,而非盲目的猜测。 我是胡博士,胡说八道的胡,但胡博士科普向来严谨。欢迎关注,欢迎捉虫。 返回搜狐,查看更多 阅读 () 作者声明 含AI生成内容 平台声明 该文观点仅代表作者本人,搜狐号系信息发布平台,搜狐仅提供信息存储空间服务。

印第安纳州奶牛场确诊首例 H5N1 病例

Indiana’s first case of H5N1 in dairy herd confirmed
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 3 日,美国农业部动植物卫生检验局 (USDA-APHIS) 在印第安纳州的一个奶牛群中确认了首例 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) H5N1 基因型 B3.13 病例。该检测是通过常规国家牛奶检测策略检测进行的,而不是移动前的 监测。这标志着印第安纳州首次报告牛感染 HPAI 病例,尽管自 2024 年 3 月以来已有 21 个州的奶牛受到感染。USDA-APHIS 正在农场进行调查、诊断测试和数据收集,以了解情况并防止进一步传播。此次检测不会改变美国农业部的 HPAI 根除策略,并且强调生物安全措施以减轻疾病的引入或传播。公众面临的风险较低,但因工作或娱乐而接触受感染鸟类或哺乳动物的个人风险较高,应遵循 CDC 的指导。来自受影响动物的牛奶正在被转移或销毁,以确保商业牛奶供应的安全,因为巴氏灭菌可以有效灭活病毒。建议生产者向州兽医报告任何出现临床症状的牲畜或异常患病或死亡的野生动物。
